<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Divine Names as "garments." It transitions from the specific attributes of Mercy and Judgment to the concept of the "Thirty Appellations" (kinuyim) which act as a veil between the Divine Essence and the world, specifically in the context of the Sanctuary and the Exile.</meta>

When He unites with Israel in holiness <note>original: עקד"ש (*a-ke-d-sh*); likely an abbreviation for "the Holy Sanctuary" (*Beit HaMikdash*) or "in Holiness" (*be-kedusha*)</note>, He removes some of His appellations <term>Kinuyim; descriptive titles or names of God (like "Merciful" or "Great") that are secondary to the essential four-letter Name</term> which are His garments. Then Israel sees the essence of the Lord <note>original: ידוד (*Y-H-V-H*); the Tetragrammaton, God’s most holy four-letter name</note>, may He be blessed, more than the other nations. Nevertheless, He is still somewhat clothed in His garments and appellations. The reason for this is that the masses are not worthy of reaching His true essence—the Name of the Lord, blessed be He—and they cannot know the ultimate depth of His being.

But when the Name, blessed be He, unites with the righteous, the pious, and the patriarchs of the world <note>Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob</note> in His full strength, He then removes all appellations from Himself. Then the Name of the Lord alone shall be exalted <note>A reference to Isaiah 2:11</note>. Thus, the Name of the Lord strips off all the appellations—which are His garments—and stands with the pious of Israel and their righteous ones. This is the secret meaning of the verse: *"I have put off my coat; how shall I put it on?"* <note>Song of Songs 5:3</note>.

The Name, blessed be He, was angry with Israel and said: "I have put off my coat"—meaning all those appellations I possess—so that the Guardian Angels of the nations of the world would not be able to take hold of Me. Thus, I remained with Israel alone. Why then do you sin and desire that I return to wearing those appellations? For if I do, the Guardian Angels of the nations will grasp those appellations and lead you into exile. Therefore He said: *"I have put off my coat; how shall I put it on?"* I have stripped from Myself the thirty appellations which are the secret of "my coat" <note>The Hebrew letter *Lamed* (ל) represents the number 30; the text suggests these 30 names form the protective "coat"</note>; why do you wish for Me to return and wear it?

*"I have washed my feet"* <note>Song of Songs 5:3</note> from the filth of the land of the nations, for *"the earth is My footstool"* <note>Isaiah 66:1</note>. How could you cause Me to lead My Divine Presence <term>Shekhinah; the indwelling presence of God in the world, often described in feminine terms</term> with you into exile in the lands of the nations, where they would be defiled by impurity? This is the secret of *"I have washed my feet; how shall I defile them?"* and the secret of *"The earth is My footstool."*

If so, consider this: when Israel went into the Babylonian exile, the Name, blessed be He, wore the appellations which are the "coat." Then the nations took hold of Him. This is what the scripture means: *"And it was in the thirtieth year"* <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note>—meaning He was "alive" <note>The Hebrew word for "alive," *Chai*, also refers to a specific divine level</note> when the Name, blessed be He, was clothed in the rest of the garments. Then: *"I was among the exiles"*—all this refers to the exile. But in the time to come, when the Divine Presence returns to its place, the Name, blessed be He, will strip off all those garments, appellations, and "wings." And then—

<column-break/>

—Israel will see the Name, blessed be He, face to face. This is the secret of the verse: *"Your teacher shall no longer be hidden [winged]"* <note>Isaiah 30:20; the Hebrew word *yikanef* (hidden) shares a root with *kanaf* (wing)</note>. From that time forward: *"Your eyes shall see your Teacher."* This means He will no longer clothe Himself in those appellations called "wings," with which the Name, blessed be He, used to cover Himself from Israel so they could not see Him. This is the meaning of *"Your teacher shall no longer be hidden."*

And this is the secret of: *"For eye to eye they shall see when the Lord returns to Zion"* <note>Isaiah 52:8</note>. It also says: *"In that day it shall be said: Behold, this is our God; we have waited for Him... let us be glad"* <note>Isaiah 25:9</note>. This "this" refers to the Lord <note>Y-H-V-H</note> with certainty, as every single person will point to Him with their finger and say, "This is the Lord!" And when will this be? At the time when He strips off the appellations and removes the "wings," so they "shall no longer be hidden."

How do we know that "wings" refers to hiding and covering? As it is said: *"And under the firmament their wings were straight, one toward the other; every one had two which covered on this side, and every one had two which covered on that side, their bodies"* <note>Ezekiel 1:23</note>. And it is said: *"Each one had six wings; with two he covered his face, and with two he covered his feet, and with two he did fly"* <note>Isaiah 6:2</note>. Flying and covering occur as one. Once the Divine Presence departed and made for itself "wings," they flew away and Israel could no longer see her. As it is written: *"And He rode upon a cherub, and did fly"* <note>Psalm 18:10</note>. This is what is meant by the statement: "The Divine Presence made ten journeys [to depart]," as is found in the tractate Rosh Hashanah <note>Mishnah Rosh Hashanah 31a-b describes the Shekhinah leaving the Temple in ten stages</note>.

### Regarding
what we have said—that the Holy Names are never erased <note>According to Jewish law, the seven primary names of God must not be erased or destroyed</note>:
If you should find it difficult and ask: "Since the Holy Names are garments, and when the Name, blessed be He, is clothed in them He is hidden and concealed from Israel, why are they not erased? Would that not be for the good of Israel and the greatness of the Lord?" This is what is meant by His saying: *"I have put off my coat; how shall I put it on?"*

### Know
that even though we say the Holy Names are not erased, they are indeed appellations that He strips off. However, they are not lost from the world. As it is said: *"He shall put off the linen garments which he put on when he went into the holy place, and shall leave them there"* <note>Leviticus 16:23, regarding the High Priest on Yom Kippur</note> forever. So too—

It addresses the legal permanence of these names by comparing them to the High Priest's garments, which are set aside but not destroyed.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the Divine Names as "garments" and "appellations." The previous page established that God "strips off" His secondary titles to be alone with Israel, but here the text explores why the Exile happened despite this closeness, linking the "thirty appellations" to the prophecy of Ezekiel and the destruction of Jerusalem.</meta>

Since there are three Names—*El*, *Elohim*, and the Lord <note>original: יד'וד (*Y-H-V-H*); the Tetragrammaton, God’s most holy four-letter name</note>—and each one carries its own descriptive titles <term>Kinuyim; appellations or secondary names of God that describe His attributes, such as "The Great" or "The Merciful"</term>, their total sum is thirty appellations. Through these thirty, the created beings are changed and governed. Therefore, it is written: *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year"* <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note>. It was through these thirty appellations that Ezekiel the prophet, peace be upon him, saw that Israel had rebelled and sinned until no merit remained for them in the Heavenly Court among any of these thirty appellations—which are thirty places of merit for Israel—to prevent the destruction of Jerusalem. Instead, from the perspective of absolute judgment, and with the agreement of *El*, *Elohim*, and the Lord, and through their thirty appellations, they all agreed in a judgment of truth that Jerusalem should be destroyed. 

This is the secret of the phrase *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year"*—that was when Jerusalem was destroyed, for it represents the "fourth attribute" <note>likely referring to the fourth Sephirah or the final letter 'H' of the Divine Name</note>, which is the fourth letter of the Name. This is the letter *He* [ה], corresponding to the "fifth of the month" <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note>. At that time, the Divine Presence <term>Shekhinah; the indwelling presence of God in the world, often personified as feminine</term> departed, which is the letter *He* called *Adonai* <note>The Name meaning "My Lord," associated with the Shekhinah</note>. Likewise, she went into exile. This is the secret of the verse: *"And I was in the midst of the exile"* <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note>. The word "And I" <note>original: ואני (*V'Ani*)</note> literally refers to the Presence itself going into exile. This is what our Sages of blessed memory said: "Israel is so beloved that wherever they were exiled, the Divine Presence went with them."

### And Now
We must enter
into the explanation of
each and every one of them, or to which family of attributes it belongs. Then you will understand the awe of the Lord, may He be blessed.

### And Before
we enter into the explanation of these
thirty appellations,
we must know that the Lord <note>Y-H-V-H</note>, may He be blessed, is the Name uniquely set apart for Israel. The other appellations are like wings or garments in which all the other Names are unified. For the seventy guardian angels of the nations are unified within these appellations and His garments. However, the Name of the Lord <note>Y-H-V-H</note> is unique to Israel alone. This is the secret of the verse: *"For you are a holy people to the Lord your God; the Lord has chosen you to be a special treasure to Himself, above all the peoples who are on the face of the earth"* <note>Deuteronomy 7:6</note>. And it is said: *"For the Lord's portion is His people"* <note>Deuteronomy 32:9</note>. This is also the secret of: *"For all the peoples walk each in the name of its god, but we will walk in the name of*

<column-break/>

*the Lord our God forever and ever"* <note>Micah 4:5</note>. This is the secret of the verse: *"And all the peoples of the earth shall see that the Name of the Lord is called upon you, and they shall be afraid of you"* <note>Deuteronomy 28:10</note>. 

The great secret was revealed by Joshua, peace be upon him, when he said: *"The Canaanites and all the inhabitants of the land will hear of it, and they will surround us and cut off our name from the earth; and what will You do for Your Great Name?"* <note>Joshua 7:9</note>. Contemplate this: the Unique Name belongs to Israel alone. This is the meaning of: *"You have affirmed the Lord... and the Lord has affirmed you"* <note>Deuteronomy 26:17-18</note>. It is found that the Name of the Lord is unique to Israel, and the name of Israel is unique to the Name of the Lord; one cannot be separated from the other. You have thus learned that the Name of the Lord is unique to Israel alone, while the other nations and their guardian angels are unified only through His "garments." 

This is the secret of: *"This is Jerusalem; I have set her in the midst of the nations and the countries that are round about her"* <note>Ezekiel 5:5</note>. I shall now explain the Name of the Lord and its portion of the earth, which is Jerusalem, and its portion of the nations, which is Israel. This is the secret of the verse: *"For Your Name is called upon Your city and upon Your people"* <note>Daniel 9:19</note>. It is also written: *"For the Lord has chosen Zion; He has desired it for His habitation"* <note>Psalm 132:13</note>. And it says: *"Blessed be the Lord out of Zion, who dwells in Jerusalem, Hallelujah"* <note>Psalm 135:21</note>. *"Out of Zion, the perfection of beauty, God has shined"* <note>Psalm 50:2</note>—meaning that from Zion He appeared to dwell in Jerusalem. This is the secret of the verse: *"For the Lord will not forsake His people for His Great Name's sake"* <note>1 Samuel 12:22</note>. This means that even though Israel may not be righteous, He will nonetheless not forsake them. The reason for this is that His Great Name is shared with them.

### And Know
that when the Name, blessed be He, appears
before the nations, He does not appear 
before them except through His garments and His ceremonial arrays <note>original: טכסיסיו (*taksisav*); from the Greek 'taxis,' referring to royal protocol or military arrangement</note>. 
But when the Name, blessed be He, stands over all of Israel, He stands with His own household and removes some of His garments, just as it is the way of a king to remove some of his garments when he is with the members of his household. This is the secret of: *"Three times a year all your males shall appear"* <note>Exodus 23:17</note>. Our Sages of blessed memory said: "Just as He comes to see, so He comes to be seen" <note>Talmud, Hagigah 2a</note>. They hinted at a great secret regarding the "Secret of the Appearance" <term>Re'ayon; the commandment to appear at the Temple during festivals</term>. They said at the beginning of the tracts *Pe'ah* and *Hagigah*: "All are obligated in the appearance." Regarding the secret of the appearance mentioned at the beginning of *Pe'ah*, even though...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the Divine Names. The previous page ended with the departure of the Divine Presence (Shekhinah) during the destruction of the Temple, linked to the "thirtieth year" of Ezekiel’s vision. Here, the author explains the mechanics of how the Divine Name moves between Mercy and Judgment, and how the thirty secondary titles (appellations) are structured within the heavenly hierarchy.</meta>

### Gates of Righteousness 9:1:80

And the Name *ADNY* <note>original: אדנ"י; Adonai, often associated with the attribute of Sovereignty</note> is entirely mercy. And when He emerges from the Name *ADNY* which is mercy—which is the "place" of the Lord <note>original: ידו"ד (*Y-H-V-H*); the Tetragrammaton</note>, blessed be He—and comes to the Name *ADNY* below, which is the Attribute of Judgment <term>Middat HaDin; the divine quality of strict justice and law</term>, and through it He judges His creatures—this provides you with the literal meaning of the scripture in its proper context. Thus, you see that all the Holy Names are suspended from the Name *Y-H-V-H*. 

And here I am entering into the explanation of the **thirty appellations** <term>Kinuyim; secondary names or descriptive titles of God, like "The Great" or "The Mighty"</term>. How are they attached to the three Names, which are *El*, *Y-H-V-H*, and *Elohim*? This is the secret of the verse: *"And it came to pass in the thirtieth year"* <note>Ezekiel 1:1</note>, for they are the secret of the thirty appellations, which are the secret of the thirty wings: ten on the right side, ten in the center, and ten on the left side. Over them are three "flags" <note>standards or divisions</note>. The middle flag is the letter *Yod* [י]. And the Name *Y-H-V-H* seals them all. 

Thus, the thirty years are found to be dependent on the Name *Y-H-V-H*. He is over all of them, they are all with Him, and everything depends on Him. We must now return and make known the secret of the order of these thirty appellations, and in which attribute each and every one of them stands. For the appellations are twenty-seven, corresponding to the twenty-seven letters <note>The 22 letters of the Hebrew alphabet plus the 5 "final" forms of letters used at the ends of words</note>, and the three primary Names are above them; thus, they all together total thirty. We are now ready to enter into this explanation, detailing each one according to the order of their levels of governance and according to the arrangement of the attributes of Mercy and Judgment.

-> And the children of Israel shall pitch their tents, every man by his own camp, and every man by his own flag, according to their hosts. <-
<note>Numbers 1:52</note>

-> Gates of Righteousness 9:1:80 <-

<column-break/>

As our Sages of blessed memory said: *"For behold, the Lord <note>original: ידו"ד</note> comes out of His place"* <note>Isaiah 26:21</note>—He leaves the Attribute of Mercy <term>Middat Rachamim; the divine quality of compassion and grace</term> and comes to the Attribute of Judgment. But what "place" could He possibly have? Does He not "measure the heavens with a span"? <note>Isaiah 40:12</note> And "where is the place of My rest?" <note>Isaiah 66:1</note> What "place" is there that we could say, "Behold, the Lord comes out of His place"? Is He not called "The Place of the World"? And is He not called "Blessed be the Place" <note>a common rabbinic title for God</note>? Indeed, our Rabbis of blessed memory said: "He is the place of the world, but the world is not His place" <note>A classic Midrashic teaching that God encompasses the universe but is not contained by it</note>.

Rather, the explanation of the verse is as follows: Behold, the Lord (*Y-H-V-H*)—which is the secret of the Name that includes both the Attribute of Mercy and Judgment—emerges from His "place," which is the Place of Mercy, and comes to the Attribute of Judgment. He "clothes" Himself in the attribute called *Elohim* <note>The Name associated with Judgment</note>. For the primary "dwelling" of the Lord (*Y-H-V-H*), blessed be He, is always within the Attribute of Mercy. This is what our Sages of blessed memory meant when they said: "God, a King who sits upon a Throne of Mercy"—He sits there always. But the Attribute of Judgment is not His primary place of dwelling; He only employs it when He is required to do so at the time of judgment.

**Furthermore**, there is a higher level to the explanation of this verse and its legendary interpretation <note>original: *Aggadah*; homiletic or narrative teachings of the Sages</note>: *"For behold, the Lord comes out of His place."* Our Rabbis said: He leaves the Attribute of Mercy and comes to the Attribute of Judgment. You already know that three Names are linked in a chain from above to below, and these are: *ADNY*, *Y-H-V-H*, and *ADNY*. The core of all these is the Name *ADNY*, and it is the place of the Lord (*Y-H-V-H*)...

It introduces the structure of the thirty divine appellations, categorized by their relationship to the Hebrew alphabet and the three primary Names of God.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the thirty descriptive titles (appellations) introduced on the previous page. The author is moving from the general concept of the "thirty years" in Ezekiel's vision to the specific linguistic and kabbalistic structure of how God’s various names relate to the primary four-letter Name, the Tetragrammaton.</meta>

The Blessed Name is destined to separate from Himself the descriptive titles <term>Kinuyim; secondary titles of God like "Great" or "Merciful" as opposed to His essential Names</term> at the time they "enter the Sanctuary," and He will leave them outside. He will no longer wear them. For these descriptive titles are the places where the "princes of the nations" <note>In Jewish thought, every nation has a presiding angelic representative or "prince" in the heavenly court</note> take hold. Yet, these princes of the nations are not entirely uprooted from existence. Therefore, the Holy Names <note>original: שמות הקדש; the primary names of God that cannot be erased according to Jewish law</note> are never erased, but the other descriptive titles that *can* be erased—such as "Merciful" and "Gracious" and their like—are in the likeness of the hosts of these princes of the nations, whom He is destined to erase from the world. 

In that future time, no nation will remain that does not reject its own "prince," as it is said: *"And many nations shall go and say: 'Come, and let us go up to the mountain of the Lord, and to the house of the God of Jacob; and He will teach us of His ways, and we will walk in His paths'"* <note>Isaiah 2:3 or Micah 4:2</note>. And it is written: *"For then will I turn to the peoples a pure language, that they may all call upon the name of the Lord, to serve Him with one accord"* <note>Zephaniah 3:9</note>. Therefore, you will find that the Holy Names which are equivalent to these titles are not erased, but those descriptive titles that are in the likeness of the nations are erased.

# And Now
We must inform you of the secret of these **thirty descriptive titles** through which the Blessed Name conducts Himself in Judgment with His creatures. We shall explain which side each title leans toward, what the specific attribute <term>Middah; a divine quality or characteristic</term> of each title is, and toward which of the Holy Names it inclines. 

Consider the sign: *"And next to him shall be the standard of the tribe of Manasseh"* <note>Numbers 2:20</note>, and it is written: *"and his host, and those that were numbered."* You find here a "standard" <note>a flag or banner</note>, a "tribe," and "those numbered." Similarly, the Lord <note>original: ידו"ד (*Y-H-V-H*); the Tetragrammaton</note>, blessed be He, is the root/essence, the Holy Names are the secret form of the standards, and the other descriptive titles are the secret of their hosts.

# And Before
Before we enter into the explanation of this, we must inform you that all the Names of the Blessed Name are included within the Name *Y-H-V-H*, blessed be He. How so? 
*   **Adonai** <note>Lord/Sovereign</note> is the secret of the "tip of the *Yod*" <note>the small stroke at the top of the first letter of the Tetragrammaton</note>.
*   The Name **Yah** [יה] is the secret of the essence of the *Yod* itself.
*   The first **Heh** [ה] of the Name *Y-H-V-H* is the secret of the Name **Elohim**.
*   The **Vav** [ו] of the Name is the secret of six names:

<column-break/>

These are three above the halfway point of the *Vav* and three below. 
1.  **El** is to the right and above the center.
2.  **Elohim** is to the left and above the center.
3.  **Y-H-V-H** is in the middle and above the center. 
4.  **Y-H-V-H Tzevaot** <note>Lord of Hosts</note> is to the right and below the center.
5.  **Elohim Tzevaot** is to the left and below the center.
6.  **Shaddai** <note>often translated as Almighty; here associated with "Living God"</note> is in the middle and below the center. 

Sometimes He is called **El Shaddai**, meaning six names are suspended from it. Furthermore, a seventh name is suspended from the letter *Vav*, which is **Eloah** <note>the word for God/Eloah sounds like the letter Vav (six) plus 'ah'</note>. The secret of this is found in the verse: *"But none saith: 'Where is Eloah my Maker [Oshai]?'"* <note>Job 35:10</note>—it does not say "He made me" [Asani], but "my Maker" [Oshai, plural/complex form]. It also says: *"God [Eloah] comes from Teman"* <note>Habakkuk 3:3</note>. 

In this way, you will also hear the words "Living God." The secret of the **final Heh** [ה] of the Name is the secret of **Adonai**, and sometimes it is called **Shaddai**. Thus, all the Holy Names are included in the Name *Y-H-V-H*, blessed be He. When we mention the Name *Y-H-V-H*, it comes with all the Holy Names, as in: *"And the Lord [Y-H-V-H] my God shall come, and all the holy ones with thee"* <note>Zechariah 14:5</note>—meaning all the Holy Names are with Thee. You have learned, then, that everything depends on the Name of the Lord. But when you mention one of the other Holy Names, it speaks only of one specific detail among the details of the Tetragrammaton, as I have informed you.

# And Now
You must know how to contemplate which letter of the Name *Y-H-V-H* a particular Holy Name refers to—such as *El*, *Elohim*, and their like. After you know this, you will understand that all individual details depend on the whole, and all Holy Names depend on the Name *Y-H-V-H*. 

If so, you will find that when one mentions one of the other Holy Names, you should understand that the Name is "clothing" Himself in a specific garment. From that name, you can understand which attribute or which garment the Name *Y-H-V-H* is wearing.
*   He may wear the garment of **El**, which is the secret of **Mercy** <term>Rachamim</term>; His "standard" testifies that He is a Merciful God.
*   He may wear the garment of **Judgment** <term>Din</term> and Sovereignty; His "standard" is **Elohim**.
*   He may wear the garment of **Justice**, which includes both Judgment and Mercy; His "standard" is **Y-H-V-H** in all His attributes. This is the secret of what the Sages said...

And when He departs from there, <term>Adad; original: אד"ד, a kabbalistic permutation of the four-letter Name of God</term>—which is Mercy and is the place of the Blessed <term>Lord; original: ידוד, a common scribal substitution for the Tetragrammaton (YHVH) to avoid writing the Name directly</term>—and comes to the name *Adonai* below, which is the Attribute of Judgment. But the Attribute of Judgment is not His primary place of dwelling at all times; rather, He utilizes it only when the Attribute of Judgment is required.

### A further, higher level explaining this verse
And the explanation in the *Aggadah* <note>The narrative, non-legal portion of Rabbinic literature</note> regarding *"For behold, the Lord comes out of His place"*: Our Rabbis of blessed memory said He departs from the Attribute of Mercy and comes to the Attribute of Judgment! You already know that three Names are held by three levels from above to below, and these are: *Adad*, the Lord (YHVH), and *Adonai*. The primary focus of all of these is the name *Adonai*, and it is the "place" of the Lord...

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the thirty divine appellations (secondary titles). Having established that these titles relate to the "princes of the nations" and the future purification of language, the author now focuses on the internal structure of these titles—specifically how they relate to the number thirty through the Hebrew alphabet and the interplay between the attributes of Mercy and Judgment.</meta>

The Blessed Name <note>God</note> is destined to separate the secondary appellations from Himself at the time they enter the Sanctuary. He will leave them outside and will no longer "clothe" Himself in them. These appellations are the places where the "Princes of the Nations" <note>the spiritual guardians or angelic representatives of the non-Jewish nations</note> take hold. However, the Princes of the Nations are never entirely uprooted; therefore, the Holy Names <note>the primary Names of God like *Elohim* or *El*</note> are never erased. 

But the other secondary appellations, which *can* be erased—such as "Merciful," "Gracious," and the like—are in the likeness of the armies of the Princes of the Nations, whom He is destined to erase from the world. No nation will remain that does not renounce its own "Prince," as it is said: *"And many nations shall go and say: 'Come, and let us go up to the mountain of the Lord, and to the house of the God of Jacob; and He will teach us of His ways, and we will walk in His paths'"* <note>Isaiah 2:3</note>. It is also written: *"For then I will turn to the peoples a pure language, that they may all call upon the name of the Lord, to serve Him with one accord"* <note>Zephaniah 3:9</note>. Therefore, you will find that Holy Names which are also used as "common" appellations <note>referring to human judges or powers</note> are not erased, but those appellations that are in the likeness of the nations are erased.

### And Now
We must make known to you the secret of these thirty appellations through which the Blessed Name conducts Himself in Judgment with His creatures. We must explain which direction each one leans, the specific attribute of every appellation, and toward which Holy Name it inclines. A sign for this is the verse: *"And by him shall be the tribe of Manasseh: and the captain of the children of Manasseh..."* <note>Numbers 2:20</note>. You will find "banners," you will find "tribes," and you will find "officers." 

In this way, the Blessed <term>Lord; original: ידוד, a scribal substitution for the Tetragrammaton YHVH</term> is the essence; the Holy Names are the secret of the form of the **Banners**, and the other secondary appellations are the secret of their **Armies**.

### And Before
we enter into the explanation of this, we must inform you that all the Names of the Blessed God are included within the Name of the Blessed <term>Lord; original: ידוד</term>. 

How so? *Adonai* <note>Lord/Master</note> is the secret of the "tittle" <note>the small stroke or point</note> of the letter *Yod*, which is the first letter of the Name. The secret of *Yah* <note>original: יה</note> is the secret of the essence of the *Yod* itself, for the letter *Yod* is the secret of *Yah*. The tittle of the *Yod* is the secret of *Adonai*. 

The secret of the first letter *He* (ה) of the Name is the secret of <term>God; original: אלדיס, a pious cipher for Elohim</term>. The secret of the letter *Vav* (ו) of the Name is the secret of six Names:

<column-break/>

These consist of three names above the midpoint of the *Vav* and three below its midpoint. These are:
1. **El** (God) on the right, above the midpoint.
2. **God** <note>original: אלדיס (Elohim)</note> on the left, above the midpoint.
3. The **Lord** <note>the Tetragrammaton</note> in the center, above the midpoint.

Then there are three below the midpoint of the *Vav*:
4. **Lord of Hosts** on the right, below the midpoint.
5. **God of Hosts** on the left, below the midpoint.
6. The **Lord** <note>the Tetragrammaton</note> in the center, below the midpoint.

Sometimes this is called *El Shaddai* <note>God Almighty</note>. Thus, we find six Names hanging upon it. Furthermore, a seventh Name is attached to the letter *Vav*, which is *Eloah* <note>God</note>. The secret of this is found in the verse: *"But none saith: Where is God my Maker"* <note>Job 35:10</note>. The word for "my Maker" <note>original: עושיי</note> is not used, but rather "Maker" <note>original: עושי</note>. It also says: *"God [Eloah] comes from Teman"* <note>Habakkuk 3:3</note>. In this way, you will also hear the words of the **Living God**. 

The secret of the final letter *He* (ה) of the Name is the secret of *Adonai*, and sometimes it is called *Shaddai*. Thus, all the Holy Names are included in the Name of the Blessed <term>Lord; original: ידוד</term>. When we mention the Name of the Lord, He comes with all the Holy Names, as it says: *"And the Lord my God shall come, and all the holy ones with thee"* <note>Zechariah 14:5</note>—meaning all the Holy Names are with You. You have therefore learned that everything depends on the Name of God. But when you mention one of the other Holy Names, it speaks only of one specific detail among the details of the Lord, as I have informed you.

### Now
it is necessary to know and to investigate which letter of the letters of the Name <note>the Tetragrammaton</note> a particular Holy Name hints at—such as *El*, *God* <note>Elohim</note>, and the like. After you know this, you will understand that all individual details depend upon the general whole, and all Holy Names depend upon the Name of the Blessed Lord. 

If this is so, you will find and learn that when one mentions one of the other Holy Names, you should understand that the Name is "clothed" in a specific "garment." From that Name, you can understand which attribute or which garment the Name of the Blessed Lord is wearing:
*   Whether it is the garment of **El**, which is the secret of **Mercy**, and His "Banner" testifies that He is a Merciful God.
*   Whether it is the garment of **Judgment and Sovereignty**, and His "Banner" is **God** <note>Elohim</note>.
*   Whether it is the garment of **Justice**, which includes both Judgment and Mercy, and His "Banner" is the **Lord** <note>the Tetragrammaton</note> in all His attributes. 

And this is the secret of what they <note>the Sages</note> said...

<meta>The text continues the systematic explanation of the Divine Names and their associated attributes (Sefirot), specifically moving from the general concept of appellations to the specific mechanics of the attribute of Lovingkindness (Chesed) and the balance of the elements. It maintains the thread of how God interacts with the world through these "garments" or titles.</meta>

"The Lord <note>original: *YHVH*</note> thunders over many waters" <note>Psalm 29:3</note> and His appellation is "Foundations," based on the foundation of the Upper Waters. Do not let it enter your mind that these are physical waters; rather, the Upper Waters are "Living Waters." Our father Abraham, peace be upon him, dug wells of water and established the attribute of **Lovingkindness** <term>Chesed: the divine attribute of expansive benevolence and mercy</term> through the Divine Name *El* <note>the primary Name of God representing mercy</note>. There are nine such appellations associated with the Name *El*. Among them is the title "Great" <note>original: *Gadol*</note>; this name represents the numerical value of 44 <note>The letters *Mem* (40) and *Dalet* (4) which spell the Hebrew word for "from/of" or link to the structure of *Chesed*</note> of Lovingkindness. "Great" is the Name that stems from the "Water," which is the attribute known to the Kabbalists as "Water within Water." This means that when you delve into this attribute, you will find mercy on every side, whether facing forward or backward. Therefore, wherever you see the word "Great," it refers to the head of the attribute of Lovingkindness. This is the secret of the clear and pure waters at the top of a vessel.

There are two sides to the attribute of "Great." The side facing upward is called **Merciful** <note>original: *Rachum*</note>, and the side facing downward is called **Gracious** <note>original: *Chanun*</note>. Even though "Merciful" and "Gracious" are both within the category of "Water within Water," the water at the top of the vessel is not identical to the water below it. The attribute of "Merciful" refers to the clear, pure waters at the very top, while the attribute of "Gracious" refers to the water immediately below them. The attribute of "Gracious" is the portion of the water that borders the "Spirit" <note>original: *Ruach*, also meaning Air or Wind</note>. In this manner, you should evaluate the order of the combinations of the Names and appellations. You will find that they are woven together according to the order of the Upper Chariot <term>Merkavah: the structure of the divine realm</term>. From here, you may enter the secret of the saying: "With the measure a person measures, so it is measured out to them." Behold, I have explained this Gate to you—how many good levels are attributed to the Omnipresent. Open your eyes to see how many hidden things and how many mysteries are concealed within the Torah. May the Lord <note>original: *YHVH*</note> teach us the wonders of His Torah, that we may grasp the secret of the unity of His faith.

## And from within

## Beauty
<note>original: *Tiferet*: the central attribute that balances mercy and judgment</note>
the keys, you will need to understand that the Blessed Name <note>the Tetragrammaton, *YHVH*</note> is the "Called One." The reason is that this Name clothes itself in all the "garments" required by the world. Sometimes it takes the attribute of **Mighty** <note>original: *Adir*</note> when showing mercy to His creatures, and sometimes...

### And After
you have known this, I must inform you of a great principle upon which one is obligated to lean, and through which you will enter the upper secrets. You must know that the Blessed Name created water. Man receives many kinds of benefits from water; even if a person touches the water itself, he is not harmed by that contact. However, if a person touches the fire itself, his body is burned, as it is said: *"Can a man scoop fire into his lap without his clothes being burned?"* <note>Proverbs 6:27</note>.

If so, behold these two elements—Fire and Water—are a great necessity in the world. For if there were no fire, nothing would be warmed and there would be no life in the world; but if there were no water, everything would be consumed by the fire. What did the Holy One, Blessed be He, do? He took a portion of the fire and a portion of the water and from them He made **Spirit** <note>original: *Ruach*, Air/Wind</note>. He then placed the Spirit between the water and the fire. Thus, the Spirit is the mediator between cold and heat—the cold coming from the water and the heat from the fire. It is found to be the middle point.

In this same way, all the other connections of the Chariot <note>the *Merkavah*</note> are formed: between Earth and Water, between Fire and Earth, between Fire and Spirit, and between Water and Spirit. Thus, the four elements circulate around each other through a mediator, and in this way they are sustained. Just as the way of the elements is below, so is the way of the elements above. Those emanations of the Upper Radiance, if there were no mediator between them, they would never be bound together, and everything would exist in a state of separation. There is no such thing as a "simple" <note>pure/unmixed</note> attribute in every respect, except from the "tip of the *Yod*" <note>the highest, most transcendent point of the Name of God</note> and above. The "middle" attributes, such as "Merciful" and "Gracious" and their like, are drawn from known sources. According to the source from which they are drawn, so is their action. I shall now explain how this is, with the help of the Blessed Name:

# The Judgment
And you should know that this is the case, for if you do not say so, one is found "cutting the saplings" <note>original: *kotzetz ba-netiot*; a Talmudic metaphor for heresy, specifically the act of separating divine attributes from their source in the Godhead</note> and separating the Unity. For if you say that the attribute of Mercy is entirely separate from the attribute of Judgment, they would never be joined; and if they were joined, the Unity would be separated. If you say that the attribute of Mercy is the opposite of the attribute of Judgment, how then can two opposites be joined as one? Know that these matters are the secret of **Emanation** <term>Atsilut: the highest of the four worlds in Kabbalah, representing the direct radiation of divine light</term> and the connection of the **Divine Spheres** <term>Sefirot: the ten creative powers or attributes of God</term>. Behold, I am enlightening your eyes:

## Know
that the attribute of **Lovingkindness** <term>Chesed</term> begins as pure, refined flour—Lovingkindness. As it spreads, it continues to thicken. This thickness is like the image of Judgment. Within that thickness, which contains a bit of Judgment, the attribute of Judgment takes hold. Yet, even the attribute of Judgment in that place of attachment is not entirely "absolute judgment," because at the point of attachment, it is intermingled with the attribute of Lovingkindness. However, as the attribute of Judgment moves further away toward the furthest edge, it becomes "strong judgment." Thus, the two ends of Lovingkindness and Judgment are strong in their respective natures, while the middle point is comprised of both and is not as strong in its specific nature as either of the two ends. This is the secret of the existing Emanations and the connection of the **Chariots** <note>original: *ha-merkavot*; the celestial structures or vehicles of divine manifestation</note>.

# The Name
The one who stands on the right side, called **El**, is the attribute of Lovingkindness, and it is the attribute of our father Abraham, peace be upon him. This is what the scripture means: "And he called there upon the name of the Lord, the Eternal God" <note>Genesis 21:33; original: "YHVH El Olam"</note>. This is the secret behind why we say "The Lord, the Great God" <note>original: "YHVH El ha-Gadol"</note>. The attribute of Lovingkindness is contained within "Water," which is the attribute of power; this is the secret of "Who lays the beams of His upper chambers in the waters" <note>Psalm 104:3</note>—His "upper chambers" literally, His "upper chambers" certainly; "But you have ascended above them all" <note>Proverbs 31:29</note>. This is the secret of **God Most High** <note>original: "El Elyon"</note> and "Blessed be God Most High, who has delivered your enemies into your hand" <note>Genesis 14:20</note>. I must awaken you to a great principle: tell yourself that there is no single attribute among all the attributes of the Holy Names that does not intermingle with its companion. For instance, you will find no attribute from Elohim and below that represents Mercy without having a bit of Judgment in it; and you will find no attribute of Judgment that does not contain Mercy. If so, you might ask: "Why then do you call it the attribute of Judgment?"

## Know
that the attribute whose essence and majority is Mercy—even though it contains a bit of Judgment—is called the "attribute of Mercy." And any attribute whose essence and majority is Judgment—even though it contains a bit of Mercy—we call it the "attribute of
<meta>catchword: Judgment</meta>

It emphasizes that while these attributes seem opposite, they are intermingled and unified within the divine structure.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the three vertical "columns" of the Sefirot. Having previously established the Right (Lovingkindness) and the Left (Judgment), the author now explains the Center column, which balances these two opposites through the attributes of Mercy, Truth, and Knowledge.</meta>

The middle element is **God** <note>original: *El*, the name associated with the right-side attribute of Lovingkindness</note> from the right side, and **Elohim** <note>original: *Al-dim*, a scribal variation to avoid writing the holy name</note> from the left side. The **Lord** <note>The Tetragrammaton, Y-H-V-H</note> is in the middle, which is **Truth**. For the word Truth <note>original: *Emet*</note> consists of the first, middle, and last letters of the alphabet <note>The letters Aleph (א), Mem (מ), and Tav (ת) are the beginning, middle, and end of the Hebrew alphabet</note>. As it is written: *“You will give truth to Jacob.”* <note>Micah 7:20</note> All of this is explained in these places.

->And this Sefirah is called<-
### Mercy
And behold, I am explaining to those who know the secret of the **Divine Chariot** <term>Merkavah: the mystical study of the heavenly realms and God’s throne</term> and those who know the secret of inner **Mercy** <term>Rachamim</term>. Through the secret of *"By knowledge, the rooms are filled,"* <note>Proverbs 24:4</note> one will understand the secret of the verse: *"I will surely remember him still; therefore my heart yearns for him; I will surely have mercy on him, says the Lord."* <note>Jeremiah 31:20</note> One who understands the secret of "mercy" will understand the secret of *"Who will be gracious to me as in the months of old,"* and the secret of the **womb** <note>The Hebrew words for "Mercy" (Rachamim) and "Womb" (Rechem) share the same root, suggesting a maternal, nurturing divine quality</note>. How many hidden treasures are within it—"He emerged from the womb." If the world were not sustained by Mercy, the child formed in the mother's womb would not survive. Everything is set in its proper place.

However, from the side of the **Supreme Crown** <term>Keter Elyon: the highest, most unknowable Sefirah</term>, it is written: *"I will be gracious to whom I will be gracious"*—implying a free gift—*"and I will show mercy to whom I will show mercy."* <note>Exodus 33:19</note> This is the secret of the emergence from the woman’s womb. After emerging from the womb, God returns to bestow goodness, placing "Merciful" before "Gracious": *"The Lord, the Lord, God, Merciful and Gracious."* Thus, Grace surrounds Mercy, both before creation and after creation. Happy are those who enter the chambers of Wisdom and merit the secrets of the Torah. Therefore, it is written: *"Your heart’s yearning and your mercies are restrained toward me."* <note>Isaiah 63:15</note> Open your eyes and see the hidden wonders in the Torah.

### And know that the attribute of
### Mercy
is tied to **Jacob**. This is why he fathered the twelve tribes. From the "yearning of the heart," he entered into the world of Mercy and opened locked doors. As it says, *"And the Lord saw that Leah was hated, and He opened her womb."* <note>Genesis 29:31</note> So too with the other barren women mentioned in scripture regarding the "opening of the womb." Therefore, the heralds of Mercy mention "Remembrance" <note>Referring to the *Zichronot* (Remembrances) prayers on Rosh Hashanah</note>, for these Remembrances are adjacent to Mercy, as in the explanation of: *"I will surely remember him still... [I will have] mercy."*

<column-break/>

Between the two names "God of Knowledge," everything is hinted at in this place. Therefore, at the beginning of the **Eighteen Blessings** <note>original: *She’alot Yach*, referring to the *Shemoneh Esrei*, the central standing prayer of Judaism</note> it says: *"You graciously grant knowledge to man."* It is also written: *"Because you have rejected knowledge, I will also reject you from being a priest to Me; and since you have forgotten the law of your God, I also will forget your children."* <note>Hosea 4:6</note> For this reason, the **Kingship** <term>Malchut: the tenth Sefirah, representing the physical world</term> is called the "Tree of Knowledge"—meaning the "Tree *of* Knowledge," just as you would say "the house of so-and-so." Everything is thus explained. Therefore, even *"a soul without knowledge is not good,"* <note>Proverbs 19:2</note> because "Good" is the extremity of the Lower Knowledge. This is the **Mighty One of Jacob**; therefore, Knowledge is in the middle. The Sages said: *"A person's knowledge should always be mingled with that of other people."* <note>Ketubot 17a; a call for social empathy and balance</note>

->And this is the Sefirah called:<-
### Truth
And behold, I am explaining: first He is called "Merciful," then "Gracious," then "Long-suffering," and then "Abundant in Kindness." If these five do not suffice, then "Truth" appears. Mercy was at the beginning of creation, as in: "He emerged from the womb." Afterward comes "Gracious," for He gives a free gift from the womb and from His own. After that, man sins. He is "Long-suffering" so that perhaps man will return in repentance. The reason for this is that the "weapons of judgment" stand at a distance; they *"come from a far country, from the end of heaven—the Lord and the weapons of His indignation."* <note>Isaiah 13:5</note> But He is *"near to all who call upon Him,"* leaning in to let His glory dwell in our land if a person merits all this. If not, his iniquities are weighed. He removes them one by one, and "Abundant in Kindness" tips the scale toward Lovingkindness. If a person still does not suffice through all this, then "let the judgment pierce the mountain" <note>A Talmudic legal maxim meaning the strict law must be followed regardless of the difficulty</note>. This is the secret of **Truth** and the final legal verdict. One who understands these secrets will understand the extent of the Blessed One's mercy on His creatures—how He judges them with mercy, but in the end, there is Justice. This is the secret of: *"And You, O Lord..."*

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the "Center Column" of the Sefirot, specifically focusing on the union between the Written and Oral Torah. It develops the concept that the Sefirah of Beauty (Tiferet) and Knowledge (Da'at) serve as the bridge between the hidden "Crown" and the revealed "Kingship."</meta>

...and it gives them, "separating the Master," <note>original: *Aluf*—this is a wordplay on the letter Aleph (א), symbolizing God as the Master of the world</note> because the **Oral Torah** <term>Torah she-be-al peh: the tradition of interpretation and legal discussion</term> has no existence except through its "kiss" with the **Written Torah** <term>Torah she-bi-khtav: the Five Books of Moses</term>. This is the true and perfect unification. And behold, the Tablets upon which the Torah was given were placed in the Ark; happy is the people for whom this is so, for in their hands is the perfect unity, and the **Unique Name** <note>the Tetragrammaton, Y-H-V-H</note> dwells among them. And this is the Sefirah mentioned in the Torah.

### You
The sign for this is the verse: *"You are awesome, You."* <note>original: *Attah*; Psalm 76:8</note> Behold, the Sefirah of the **Crown** <term>Keter: the highest, most hidden divine emanation</term> is called:

### He
This is because it cannot be grasped at all <note>original: *Hu*; the pronoun "He" implies a distant, third-person hiddenness</note>. However, the **Middle Line** is called **You** <note>original: *Attah*; a second-person address implying a closer relationship</note>, which the prophets perceive through the attribute of **Kingship** <term>Malchut: the lowest Sefirah, through which divine energy enters our world</term>. And the attribute of Kingship is called:

### I
This is because it is accessible to those who possess the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: *Ani*; the first-person pronoun implies the most direct, immanent presence</note>. These matters are among the "secrets of the world," and whoever has eyes may see. This Sefirah is also called:

### Awesome
The sign for this is: *"Awesome in praises, doing wonders."* <note>original: *Nora*; Exodus 15:11</note> This is the attribute of **Jacob**. When he attached himself to it, it is written: *"And he was afraid and said: How awesome is this place!"* <note>Genesis 28:17</note> This is the secret of "The Great, the Mighty, and the **Awesome**"—Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, representing **Greatness** <note>Chesed/Lovingkindness</note>, **Might** <note>Gevurah/Judgment</note>, and **Beauty** <note>Tiferet/Harmony</note>. It is called "Awesome" because within it all awesome things and all "looking glasses" <note>prophetic visions</note> are seen. I am hinting at this: *"I will praise You, for I am awesomely and wonderfully made."* <note>Psalm 139:14</note> This is the secret of the connection of the Sefirot through "Awesomeness." And this Sefirah is also called:

### Knowledge
I shall now explain the connection of the upper Sefirot: **Wisdom**, **Understanding**, and **Crown** <note>Chochmah, Binah, and Keter</note>. Behold, **Knowledge** <term>Da'at</term> is the secret of the Middle Line, which takes hold of both the Crown and Kingship, and everything is founded upon it. This is the secret of: *"By wisdom, by understanding, and by knowledge."* I will now explain the verse: *"By knowledge the rooms are filled with all precious and pleasant riches."* <note>Proverbs 24:4</note> And it is written: *"By His knowledge the depths were broken up, and the skies drop down the dew."* <note>Proverbs 3:20</note> And it is written: *"For the Lord is a God of knowledge..."* <note>1 Samuel 2:3</note> and so on. One who knows the secret of Knowledge, which is the secret of the Middle Line, will understand the secret of what our Sages of blessed memory said: "Great is knowledge, for it was placed..."

<meta>catchword: between</meta>

<column-break/>

...as an example, using the name of the **Attribute of God** <note>original: *Elohim*, the name associated with strict justice</note> when He judges all creatures, and likewise with all other names. This is the secret of **Beauty** <term>Tiferet</term>. The great principle is: *"And you shall make holy garments for Aaron your brother, for honor and for beauty."* <note>Exodus 28:2</note> Like a bridegroom acting as a priest in **beauty** <note>original: *Pe'er*</note>. The great secret lies in these "branches," as it is said: *"And it sent out branches."* <note>Ezekiel 17:6</note> Behold, the name of **Beauty** rises up to the **Supreme Crown** and descends to **Kingship**, taking hold of all sides. Therefore, it is written: *"Yours, O Lord, is the greatness, the might, the beauty, the victory, and the majesty."* <note>1 Chronicles 29:11</note> Because of this, it is called the "Middle Line." We have already explained its significance above regarding the secret of the letter *Vav* <note>The letter Vav (ו) has the numerical value of 6 and represents the center column connecting the upper and lower realms</note> of the Unique Name with six names. And this attribute is called **Justice** <term>Mishpat</term>. The secret is: *"You have executed justice and righteousness in Jacob."* <note>Psalm 99:4</note> There is no "Justice" that does not contain **Mercy**, for He judges His world with righteousness. "Let the law pierce the mountain" <note>A legal maxim meaning the law must be strictly applied</note>; therefore, He commanded the **Great Sanhedrin** <term>The supreme religious and legal assembly of ancient Israel</term> that they should not show [excessive] mercy in judgment, but rather said: *"And they shall judge the people with righteous judgment."* <note>Deuteronomy 16:18</note> But the Blessed Name *"The Lord of Hosts is exalted in justice, and the Holy God is sanctified in righteousness."* <note>Isaiah 5:16</note> Deep secrets are hinted at in this verse for those who know the hidden things of the Torah. **King David**, peace be upon him, before he sinned, said: *"As for me, I will behold Your face in righteousness."* <note>Psalm 17:15</note> But after he sinned, he said: *"Enter not into judgment with Your servant."* <note>Psalm 143:2</note>

### And Know
That were it not for the "Justice" that is unified within the **Righteous One** <note>The Sefirah of Yesod</note>, creatures would have no existence through the law of "Righteousness." Therefore it says: *"Righteousness and justice are the foundation of Your throne; mercy and truth go before Your face."* <note>Psalm 89:15</note>

### And Know
That "Righteousness" and "Justice" are the secret of **Kingship** and **Beauty**. Kingship is Law/Judgment, while Beauty is Law/Judgment combined with Mercy. This is the Sefirah called the **Written Torah**. **Moses our teacher**, peace be upon him, received it, for there is no way for any other creature to receive it except for Moses, who entered the innermost Palace and merited to serve within, regarding the matter of: *"And the Lord spoke to Moses face to face."* <note>Exodus 33:11</note> The **Oral Torah** is Kingship. Therefore, one does not write down the Oral Torah <note>Referencing the ancient prohibition against writing the oral tradition</note> so as not to establish for it a place separate from the Written Torah, because...

<summary>This page explains the Sefirot of the Middle Column (Tiferet, Da'at, and Keter) through divine pronouns and biblical figures, emphasizing the balance between Justice and Mercy. It identifies the Written Torah with the attribute of Beauty (Moses) and the Oral Torah with Kingship.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the divine names and their role in the structure of the universe, following the previous explanation of the Sefirot (Divine Emanations) as "He," "You," and "I." It transitions from the union of the Written and Oral Torah to the specific power of the name Elohim in the Work of Creation.</meta>

The **Work of Creation** <term>Ma'aseh Bereshit: the mysteries of the universe's origin</term> used the name **God** <note>original: *Elohim*</note> to teach that everything was made according to **Law** <term>Din: divine justice, structure, and natural law</term>. Therefore, it is not necessary to change any of the natural patterns of the world in order to perfect it. However, it is necessary to change nature at times to demonstrate His kingdom and His dominion over all created beings—to make it known that He made everything and everything is in His hand.

# Therefore, you will find in the psalm
of the **Great Hallel** <note>Psalm 136, a prayer of thanksgiving</note> three names: **The Eternal**, **God**, and **Lord** <note>original: Y-H-V-H, Elohim, Adonai</note>. As it says: *“Give thanks to the Eternal for He is good... Give thanks to the God of gods... Give thanks to the Lord of lords.”* Truly, the first name represents the essence of governance and the existence of the **Ancient Name** <note>referring to God’s most primordial, hidden aspect</note>, may He be blessed, whose thoughts are deep in secret; as it says, *“To Him who alone does great wonders.”* Then it returns to the second name: *“Give thanks to the God of gods.”* The psalm explains this by saying: *“To Him who made the heavens with understanding... to Him who spread out the earth above the waters... to Him who made great lights... the sun to rule by day... the moon and stars to rule by night.”* Then it returns to the third name, which is **Lord** <note>original: *Adonai*</note>, and it explains the secret of **Lordship** <term>Adonut: God’s mastery and active control over the world</term> and the dominion and governance over all creatures. This is as the scripture says: *“Whatever the Eternal pleases, He does in heaven and on earth.”* And it says: *“He does according to His will in the army of heaven, and no one can restrain His hand or say to Him, ‘What have You done?’”* <note>original: a paraphrased Aramaic quote from Daniel 4:32</note>. To correspond to the name **Lord**, the text returns to complete the third section in the psalm: *“To Him who struck Egypt in their firstborn... and brought Israel out from among them... with a strong hand and an outstretched arm... to Him who divided the Red Sea into parts... and made Israel pass through the midst of it... but overthrew Pharaoh and his army in the Red Sea.”* And so goes the rest of the psalm. Therefore, it is called the **Great Hallel**, because it includes the **Ancient Name** <note>Y-H-V-H</note>, may He be blessed, in the verse *“Give thanks to the Eternal for He is good.”* It also includes the name **God** <note>Elohim</note>, through which the world was created according to the **Upright Law**, such that nothing needs addition or subtraction, according to the design that the Name, may He be blessed, understood; this is why it says *“to the God of gods.”* And it includes the name **Lord** <note>Adonai</note>, which is the name indicating dominion and mastery over all created things, and it is in His hand to change nature

<column-break/>

and to do with it His desire and His will, as follows, for He is the one who created them. Therefore, this psalm is called the **Great Hallel**—the head of all types of praises. You will find within it twenty-six times the phrase *“For His mercy endures forever,”* <note>original: *Ki L’olam Chasdo*</note> corresponding to the numerical value of the **Divine Name** <note>the name Y-H-V-H equals 26 in Gematria</note>. We have already explained in the **Fifth Gate** <note>referring to an earlier section of this manuscript or a referenced work</note> that between the **Will** <note>original: *Chephetz*</note> and the name **God** <note>Elohim</note> stands the **Great Sanhedrin** <term>Sanhedri Gedola: here referring to a celestial assembly of powers</term>, and there is the life-force of **Judgment** <note>Din</note> according to the truth.

# And know that all the Work of Creation
and the secrets of the **Sefirot** <term>Sefirot: the ten divine emanations or attributes in Kabbalah</term> are sealed with the name **God** <note>Elohim</note> from beginning to end, in the foundation of the initial combination of letters at the creation of the world. The **Central Point** upon which every letter combination is based, as recounted in the **Book of Formation** <term>Sefer Yetzirah: an ancient mystical text on the creation of the universe through letters and numbers</term>, is the secret of **God the Eternal**. Therefore, our father Abraham, peace be upon him, made this known to all the inhabitants of the world when he *“called there upon the name of the Eternal, the Everlasting God.”* This is the first of all the descriptions attached to the Name of the Name, may He be blessed. When you remember regarding the name **God** <note>original: *El*</note> that it is the secret of the ten levels of the **Sefirot** and the **Ten Sayings** <note>the ten utterances in Genesis</note> through which the world was created, you will find twelve <note>meaning unknown; possibly referring to a specific letter combination</note> from Aleph to Yod. Within them are included all created beings and the accompanying secret: *“Your God, the heavens and the heaven of heavens, and the earth and all that is in it.”* Therefore, it says: *“All the rivers run into the sea.”* This refers to the literal **Sea**, which is the "wing" of **God**. However, the sea is never full, because everything depends on the **Name**, which is the Foundation and the Essence. Therefore, He made known in the Torah that when the name **God** <note>Elohim</note> has its **Seal** removed from it—which is the "Soul" from His **Great Name** <note>Y-H-V-H</note>—what remains is **Mute** <note>original wordplay: Elohim (אלהים) minus the divine spirit/letters leaves the word *Ilem* (אלם), meaning "mute" or "silent"</note>. It can no longer speak or do anything. This is the meaning of: *“Who makes a man mute? Is it not I, the Eternal?”* When the name **Eternal** <note>Y-H-V-H</note> stands alone, it takes away the **Emanation**, the **Abundance**, and the **Soul** that it had placed into the name **God** <note>Elohim</note>, and thus it remains **Mute**. Therefore, it says: *“Give thanks to the Eternal for He is good”*—He did not change it—but when it said *“Give thanks to the God of gods,”* it doubled the words; and so too with *“Give thanks to the Lord of lords.”* This is because He is not "One" except through the aspect of the Name of the Name, may He be blessed. Therefore: *“He who sacrifices to any god shall be utterly destroyed, save unto the Eternal only.”* <note>Exodus 22:19</note>

<summary>This page explores the mystical significance of the divine names Y-H-V-H, Elohim, and Adonai as they appear in the Great Hallel (Psalm 136). It explains that while nature is governed by fixed law (Elohim), God retains the lordship (Adonai) to perform miracles, and that the power of all divine attributes is derived solely from the singular essence of the Eternal Name.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the exploration of the Sefirot (divine emanations) and their relationship to biblical descriptions of God. It specifically reconciles the apparent contradiction between Moses speaking to God "face to face" and God’s statement that "no man shall see My face and live," while also transitioning into a study of the Name *Elohim*.</meta>

"The Eternal is with you," and it did not say "Face to Face" <note>original: *panim el panim*</note> but rather "Face *within* Face" <note>original: *panim be-panim*</note>, meaning a face encountered through the way of the Face. This is the meaning of "Behold, I come to you in a thick cloud" and "out of the midst of the fire," and everything is explained. We have more to add for you regarding the conclusion of this Sefirah. It is written: "His legs are pillars of marble" <note>Song of Songs 5:15</note>. His "legs" refer to **Eternity** and **Splendor** <term>Netzach and Hod: the seventh and eighth Sefirot, representing the "supports" of the divine structure</term>. "Pillars of marble" <note>original: *shesh*, which means marble but also the number six</note> are **Beauty** <term>Tiferet: the central Sefirah, associated with the number six because it unites six directions</term>, which is the secret of the Six Names. After mentioning the legs (Netzach and Hod) which are the "Pillars of Six"—the secret of Jachin and Boaz, the two pillars of the Temple—he mentions the attributes of **Foundation** and **Kingdom** <term>Yesod and Malchut: the ninth and tenth Sefirot</term>. He says afterward: "founded on pedestals of fine gold." One who understands the secret of these and their principles will enter into many "Chariots" <note>mystical visions of the divine realm</note>. Upon this Foundation, the Holy Temple was built with two pillars, and the "bowls of the capitals" on top of the pillars were two, and the "networks" were two, to cover the two bowls of the capitals—all corresponding to the order of the Sefirot above and below. You will see in this further secrets and hidden treasures; see, I have opened for you the gates of knowledge of the Blessed Name, the Unique One.

---

The **Work of Creation** <term>Ma'aseh Bereshit: the mysteries of the origins of the universe</term> was performed according to Truth and Perfect Wisdom in all creatures after their kind, among those in the higher realms and those in the lower. So too are the limbs of every single creature; though they differ from one another, they are all made with absolute perfection and the proper rectitude <term>Tikkun: the designed state of completeness</term> required for each creature and each limb. This is what Solomon, peace be upon him, said: "Whatever God does, it shall be forever; nothing can be added to it, and nothing taken from it" <note>Ecclesiastes 3:14</note>. The reason is that the Blessed Name made them all with absolute perfection; nothing in the world can be more perfectly formed than the nature prepared by the Blessed and Exalted Name. Therefore, it is mentioned in every "Work..."

<column-break/>

"...I will surely have mercy on him," says the Eternal. Abraham took hold of the attribute of **Kindness** <term>Chesed: divine benevolence</term>, and Jacob held the attribute of **Mercy** <term>Rachamim: the compassionate balance between kindness and justice</term>. Behold, the scripture proclaims: "The Eternal is good to all, and His mercies are over all His works," for without mercy, no creature could be born into the world. This Sefirah is called **Face** <note>original: *Panim*</note>. The secret of this is: "And you shall see my back, but my face shall not be seen." "Back" refers to the secret of **Kingdom** <term>Malchut: the lowest Sefirah, through which humans perceive a 'reflection' of God</term>. Therefore, "He called to Moses and the Eternal spoke to him." The Blessed Name did not withhold from Moses his request, but He told him that he did not have the power to endure it. The Blessed Name is good and does good, granting the requests of His pious ones; therefore, He said: "My face *shall not be* seen" <note>Exodus 33:23</note>—He did not say "You *must* not see My face." "They shall not be seen" means it is impossible for a human to look upon them. Even if He gave permission, you would be unable to see. As for the phrase "And the Eternal spoke to Moses face to face," it means there was no interruption between them, and He was able to speak with him face to face. Yet, due to the magnitude of the Radiance <term>Zohar: the overwhelming divine light</term>, Moses could not look at the Face itself. Consider the parable of a person seeing the "eye" of the sun; he knows it is there, but he cannot look directly into it. So it is with "You cannot see My face," because the sight of the eyes is weak. Thus, "face to face" He spoke...

---

# The Name God
This is the sixth of the Sefirot <note>original: *Elohim*. In this system, the author counts upwards from the bottom, making Gevurah (Judgment) the sixth gate</note>. We have already explained to you in the Fifth Gate the nature of the levels by way of the Name of the Blessed Name—how He clothes Himself in names and epithets according to the need to conduct Himself with the inhabitants of the world. By whatever attribute He needs to act, by that attribute He is named at that moment.

## And Now
You must know that the attribute of **Strength** <term>Gevurah: the Sefirah of discipline and power</term> is called **God** <note>original: *Elohim*</note>. This represents Absolute Judgment <term>Din: strict law and justice</term> in which there is no change. Therefore, the world was created with the Name *Elohim*, to say that every...

<meta>This page continues the technical exploration of the Sefirah (divine attribute) of Gevurah (Strength/Judgment). While the previous page discussed the hierarchy of "Heaven" and "Earth" in the emanative process, this page defines specific titles and qualities of Gevurah, such as "Rock," "Wealth," and "Arm," focusing on how Judgment is tempered by Mercy during the High Holy Days.</meta>

is judged, and the necessity of the servants to be a "flaming fire" <note>A reference to Psalm 104:4, "His ministers a flaming fire." In Kabbalah, the "servants" or angels of this realm are associated with the heat of divine Judgment.</note>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the attribute of Gevurah (Might/Judgment). While the previous page introduced titles like "Rock" and "Wealth," this page explores the association of this Sephirah with Fire, the "Fear of Isaac," and the North, explaining how strict Judgment interacts with the giving of the Torah and the purification of sinners.</meta>

...his impulse, then he is worthy to enter this palace and all his desires will be fulfilled. Therefore, it is asked: "From when do we mention the Might of the Rains?" <note>original: "גבורת גשמים" (Gevurat Geshamim). A reference to the Mishnah, Berakhot 5:2; rain is considered an expression of God's power and judgment.</note> This means, when they descend with strict Judgment <term>Din (דין) — the attribute of divine justice and limitation</term>, whether the world is worthy or not. And this is the secret of all the hints in the Torah, as it is said: "I will give the rain of your land in its season" <note>Deuteronomy 11:14</note>, or "the treasury of the heavens." Therefore, it is judged with Might, and therefore those who stand in this place are called "Mighty Ones," for it is a place of Dread. This is the secret of: "Sixty mighty men surrounding it, of the mighty of Israel... all of them clutching a sword, skilled in warfare, because of the dread in the nights" <note>Song of Songs 3:7-8</note>. Everything is hinted at in this place. 

And behold, Moses our Teacher, peace be upon him, ascended to this place and took the Torah by force: "A wise man scales the city of the mighty" <note>Proverbs 21:22</note>, for the Torah was given from the Right hand <note>representing Hesed/Kindness</note> by way of the Left hand <note>representing Gevurah/Might</note>. And this is the secret of "And God spoke all these words" <note>Exodus 20:1</note>. In Might the Torah was given, and it was drawn from the Right by way of the Left and given to Moses. This is the explanation for all we have explained above; understand this.

### And this Sephirah
### is called
### Fire
The Great <note>original: "Esh" (אש)</note>, because this fire is stronger than any fire in the world, and all types of fire are like straw before it. It is that which licks and burns, and fights and causes Judgment. And because the Torah was given from its source, it was given from the midst of the fire: "At His right hand was a fiery law for them" <note>Deuteronomy 33:2</note>. And it is written: "You heard His words out of the midst of the fire" <note>Deuteronomy 4:36</note>. And it is written: "Let me not see this great fire any more, that I die not" <note>Exodus 20:19</note>. Therefore, it says they all died and their souls departed when He spoke to them, for no creature has the power to stand before the Great Judgment of those who depend on Might. From this place, all types of fire are drawn. One verse says: "Who makes His angels spirits, His ministers a flaming fire" <note>Psalm 104:4</note>. The angels are spirit, therefore they are a "stormy wind" and a "pure spirit"; but His ministers are fire, as it is said: "For by fire will the Lord be 

<column-break/>

judged" <note>Isaiah 66:16</note>. What we have explained is the opposite of how we erred in the literal meaning of the text, as many people do.

## And Know
And know that in this place there is a receptacle, a foothold, and success for the wicked, in order to chastise the sinners from among them. This is the meaning of: "And Isaac loved Esau because he had a taste for game in his mouth" <note>Genesis 25:28</note>. And the secret of: "Now therefore take, I pray thee, thy weapons, thy quiver and thy bow" <note>Genesis 27:3</note>. God forbid that Isaac acted in his own mind to bless the wicked Esau with the blessing intended for Jacob, which is the blessing of Abraham. The end of the matter proves its beginning. 

One who understands the secret of the "tastes" <note>or "accents"</note> that we explained regarding the word "North" <note>Tzafon (צפון)</note> will understand the secret of the sacrifices called "Holy of Holies," whose slaughter is in the North, and the receiving of their blood in a vessel of service is in the North: "And he shall slaughter it on the side of the altar northward" <note>Leviticus 1:11</note>. "The sacrifices of God are a broken spirit" <note>Psalm 51:19</note>. And since this *Sephirah* called North <term>Tzafon (צפון) — also meaning 'hidden'</term> prevents the sinners from completing their desire, it is written: "upon the hollow of his thigh... a hand grew faint." <note>An interpretive reading of Genesis 32:25</note>. And the secret is the "robber" <note>or "one who takes"</note> on whom all depend, and "He had rays flashing from His hand, and there was the hiding of His power" <note>Habakkuk 3:4</note>. "The hiding of His power" certainly, for it is a treasury where the requests of all sinners are hidden. This *Sephirah* is found called by this name in every place.

## Fear
"Fear" <note>original: "Pachad" (פחד)</note>—"The Fear" of the proverb, and "Fear of His people" was there, "Fear in Zion." This is the secret of "The Fear of Isaac" <note>Genesis 31:42</note>, and "Dread in the nights," and "My flesh trembles for fear of You" <note>Psalm 119:120</note>—all of this is a hint. And this *Sephirah* is called:

## Might
"Might" <note>original: "Gevurah" (גבורה)</note> in the secret of "Yours, O Lord, is the Greatness and the Might" <note>1 Chronicles 29:11</note>. That is to say, the place of the hard, strong, great Judgment where no creature can enter because of the strength of the Judgment, except for the "Mighty Ones" who are named after it. This is the secret of the saying: "Who is mighty? He who conquers his impulse" <note>Pirkei Avot 4:1</note>.
